Document 002: 10年期国债收益率创2002年最高，法国会是欧债危机2.0的引爆点吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/10年期国债收益率创2002年最高，法国会是欧债危机2.0的引爆点吗？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

法国财政恶化与社会动荡交织，债券市场⻛险警报持续升级： 10 年期国债收益率⼀度触及
5% ，创 2002 年以来新⾼，法德利差逼近 160 个基点。穆迪评级审查临近， 2027 年预算案
推进受阻，外国投资者加速撤离。分析⼈⼠警告，法国或成为欧元区新⼀轮主权债务危机
的⻛险源，财政紧缩与政治博弈也将考验欧洲整体稳定。
摘要：
法国正同时承受社会动荡与财政恶化的双重压⼒，债券市场已率先作出反应。
法国 10 年期国债收益率周三早间⼀度升⾄ 5% ，创 2002 年以来最⾼⽔平。
与此同时，法德 10 年期国债利差逼近 160 个基点，触及欧债危机以来最⾼点，外国投资者正加速
撤离法国国债市场。
市场的紧张情绪叠加多重催化剂：上周末爆发的极左翼团体骚乱、本周晚些时候即将到来的穆
迪主权评级审查，以及政府在推进 2027 年预算案上的持续受阻。德意志银⾏⾼级经济学家
Yacine Rouimi 在研究报告中指出，在 18 个欧元区成员国中，法国是⾯临主权债务危机⻛险最⾼
的经济体。
骚乱失控，内政部⻓请辞遭拒

上周末，法国多个⼤城市爆发由极左翼团体主导的街头骚乱，暴徒冲击并洗劫零售商店及奢侈
品⻔店，纵⽕焚烧建筑，并与警⽅发⽣激烈冲突。
内政部⻓ Laurent Nuñez 周⼀公开承认，警⽅在部分⼤都市区⼀度失去对局势的控制。 Nuñez 随
后向总统⻢克⻰提出辞职，但遭到后者拒绝。
花旗集团策略师 Jamie Searle 警告称， "OAT 与德债利差昨⽇已告破位 " ，法国的国别⻛险溢价正
进⼊ " 未知领域 " 。Searle 同时指出，上周末的骚乱进⼀步加剧了法国预算谈判的复杂程度。
财政积弊深重，评级压⼒迫近
法国的财政困境并⾮⼀朝⼀⼣。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
《世界报》编辑总监、英国《⾦融时报》专栏作家 Sylvie Kauffmann 撰⽂指出，法国⾃ 1974 年
以来从未实现财政平衡，新冠疫情后 " 不惜⼀切代价 " 的财政扩张政策更将公共⾚字推升⾄今年
GDP 的 5.4% 。⽬前，法国国家债务已达 GDP 的 119% ，为 1946 年以来最⾼⽔平，且仍在持续攀
升。
穆迪定于本周晚些时候对法国 Aa3 主权评级展开审查，该评级此前已被列⼊负⾯展望。在政治僵
局之下，⻢克⻰政府能否顺利通过 2027 年财年预算案，仍存在较⼤不确定性。债券市场的定价
已在相当程度上反映出对评级下调的担忧。
欧债危机 2.0 ⻛险浮现
德意志银⾏经济学家 Rouimi 构建的欧盟经济动荡⻛险评估框架涵盖公共财政、对外收⽀、私⼈
债务、银⾏业韧性及劳动⼒市场等多个维度。

评估结果显⽰，爱尔兰、希腊和葡萄⽛⾃ 2010 年以来⻛险状况显著改善，⽽法国、⽐利时和芬
兰则持续朝错误⽅向演变。 Rouimi 的结论是，⼀旦欧元区再度爆发系统性危机，法国可能成为
新的震源。
花旗策略师 Searle 亦对此表⽰担忧。外国投资者持续减持法国国债的趋势，正在加剧市场对流动
性和融资成本的顾虑。
福利削减难题，⼤选前景⽣变
分析⼈⼠指出，此轮骚乱的深层逻辑与法国福利体系的可持续性危机密切相关。在⾼利率环境
下，如何为庞⼤的福利国家持续融资，已成为法国财政政策的核⼼⽭盾。若不推⾏实质性财政
整顿，投资者信⼼的进⼀步流失将难以避免。
然⽽，任何紧缩措施都将⾯临来⾃左翼的强烈抵制，⽽这恰恰为极右翼候选⼈ Marine Le Pen 在
明年总统⼤选中提供了有利的⺠调空间。当前的多重危机，正在重塑法国政治格局，并对欧元
区整体稳定构成潜在威胁。

### Page 1

本刊載之報告為元富投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，
審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬元富投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞科(2408) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成

一、南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造廠，2026 年資本支出
上修至 697 億元(YoY+420%) 
南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造商 ， 最大股東為台塑集團之
南亞塑膠持有 29.28%股權，主要競爭對手包含
Samsung、SK Hynix、Micron 以及華邦電，目前月產能
為 6.5 萬片，20nm 和 1B 為主力生產製程。依產品終端
應用區分，消費性電子和 AI 分別占營收比重為 60%+和
20%+。公司維持 2026 年銷售量年增 15-20%目標，因
5A 新廠需待 2H27 量產且存貨處於低水位 ， 故銷售量年
增率不如去年亮眼。至於在資本支出方面，2026 年資本
支出由 520 億元上修至 697 億元，其中五成將用於生產
設備 。 此外 ， 新廠將於1Q27 開始裝機 ，2H27 開始量產，
2028 年達成產能 30K， 新廠全產能由45K 上修至 50K，
投入資金為 4800 億元。 
二、客戶新 LTA 生效致使 ASP 季增幅超過六成，3Q26 
25.4 元續創新高 
3Q26 營收 1336 億元(+62%QoQ)，受惠於 LTA 長約價
格調漲，ASP 季增超過六十位數百分比，銷售量和匯率
則分別為季減中個位數和季增低個位數 ， 受惠於ASP 大
幅上揚且折舊費用持穩 ， 毛利率85.7 優於前季之 79.5%，
營業利益 1068.9 億元(+76%QoQ)，稅後淨利 876.2 億
元(+75%QoQ)，EPS 25.4 元續創新高。 
三、DRAM 供不應求延續，但 ASP 增幅將收斂，估計
4Q26 EPS 28.13 元 
DRAM 產業供不應求持續 ， 原廠供給持續向AI 傾斜 ， 由
於合約價已大幅調漲，ASP 增幅將收斂，估計 4Q26 營
收 1474.1 億元(+10.3%QoQ)，毛利率改善至 86.6%，
營業利益 1196.5 億元(+12%QoQ)，稅後淨利 970.4 億
元(+11%QoQ)，EPS 28.13 元。  
四、原廠新產能規劃搭配客戶長約需求，且 HBM 投片
占比增加，2027 年產業供不應求已成定局  
在產業需求方面，全球九大 CSP 2027 年 Capex 1.38
兆美元(+50%YoY)，AI 運算將主導 DRAM 需求成長，
帶動 DRAM 需求從 HBM、LPDDR5 擴展至網路 、 儲存，
至於傳統應用則面臨供給不足以及價格調漲，導致產品
降規或生命周期延長，部分低階市場需求則需仰賴回收
DRAM。在產業供給方面，雖供應商整體擴充產能，但
產能規劃將搭配客戶長約需求，有助長期供需穩定，加
計 HBM 於產品投片占比由今年 22%增加至明年之 30%，
致使 DRAM 新增產能主要用於 HBM 生產。故以整體市
況而言，AI 強勁需求將推動記憶體供需結構改變，降低
景氣循環對於供應商影響，產業庫存仍偏低，2027 年產
業供不應求可望延續。 
在 DRAM 報價走勢上 ， 雖消費端壓力未減 ， 但AI Server
增量需求支撐 4Q26 記憶體合約價漲勢 ， 集邦預估4Q26
傳統型 DRAM 價格季增 10-15%低於前季之 13-18%，
就南亞科而言 ， 營收占比達50%+的 LTA 客戶已於 3Q26
大幅調漲價格，故預期 4Q26 價格漲勢將趨緩。 
五、新廠產出效益於 2H27 開始發酵，長期 AI 產能占比
上看五成 
南亞科今年資本支出從 520 億元上修至 697 億元，主因
為生產設備投資增加，5A 新廠維持 1Q27 裝機規劃，
2028 年和 2030 年投片目標分別為 30K 和 50K， 整體新
廠投資規模達 4800 億元 ， 由於5A 新廠量產預計於 2H27，
故 4Q26-2Q27 出貨量約當 3Q26，至於屏東 3D 晶圓封
裝測試廠預期在 2028 年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)和晶圓堆
疊等先進技術。此外，儘管資本支出積極，目前AI 基礎
設施(包含 Server、網路、儲存以及系統管理)客戶營收
占比超過 20%， 但新產能擴建主要以AI 客戶需求為主，
故經營階層預期 2029-2030 年 AI 營收占比將上看
40-50%。 
六、維持買進理由 
1)預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 75.63 元和
119.26 元。 
2)考量 2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成，維持買進評等，TP 660 元 (2027 PBR x 2.8)。

Document 091: 261012_daiwa_nytech

Source PDF: all report pdf/261012_daiwa_nytech.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-12

Status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Nanya Technology (Nanya) announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 
12 October. Its revenue of TWD133.6m (+62% QoQ, +612% YoY) and operating profit of 
TWD106.9m (+76% QoQ, +95.5x YoY) were robust. While shipments decreased by a mid-
single-digit % QoQ, ASP increased by > 60% QoQ mainly due to renewed LTA contracts. 
While DDR4 accounted for >60% of revenue, management expects the favourable pricing 
environment to persist on robust demand and supply constraints. However, due to massive 
capex (c.USD16bn) on the build-out of a new 50k wpm capacity, management didn’t 
announce an enhanced shareholder return plan or a higher dividend payout.

Key highlights

• 3Q26 results review. Nanya's 3Q26 revenue of TWD133.6m (+62% QoQ, +612% YoY) 
and operating profit of TWD106.9m (+76% QoQ, +95.5x YoY) were slightly below our 
and recent market estimates due to higher employee compensation expenses. Its 
DRAM ASP increased by >60% QoQ in 3Q26 on LTA price adjustments, while 
shipments decreased by a mid-single-digit % QoQ. DDR5 accounted for >10% of 3Q26 
revenue, while DDR4 accounted for >60%. Management guided for a persistent market 
shortage and a continued price uptrend in 4Q26, while shipments are expected to 
remain flat until wafer output from the new fab comes online in 4Q27.

• Key takeaways from conference call. Management expects AI infrastructure to keep 
driving DRAM demand. While AI infrastructure and server applications currently account 
for >20% of revenue, it expects this exposure to grow to >30% through 2029-30. In 
particular, DDR4 demand now accounts for 12-14% of total DRAM market demand, 
maintaining the higher ASP trend on strong demand. With major DRAM players having 
almost completed their HBM supply negotiations for 2027, management believes it 
would be hard to increase supply given capacity allocation and longer lead times. Its 
LTA output portion was >20%, and management expects this to increase going forward. 
The company announced a new fab construction plan for 30k wpm by the end of 2028 
and up to 50k wpm by 2030 to respond to LTA demand from AI infrastructure. Despite 
the solid 3Q26 earnings, there was no announcement of an increased dividend payout, 
due to the capex burden (c.USD16bn for 50k wpm). It did not guide for any share 
buyback.

Recommendation 
We have a Buy (1) call on Nanya with a 12M TP of TWD700, based on a 7.4x PER on the 
2026-27E EPS. While the robust 3Q26 earnings led by the significant ASP hike was 
positive, we view the delay in announcing a shareholder return plan as disappointing. Key 
risk: weaker-than-expected commodity DRAM demand due to delays in data centre build-
outs.

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Nanya Technology
Further upside to expectations likely
Another strong quarter
Nanya Tech (NTC) reported strong 3Q26 revenue of NT$133.6bn (+62% QoQ,
+612% YoY) in line with UBSe of NT$132.7bn and 6% above Visible Alpha consensus
of NT$126.5bn. EPS came in at $25.40, slightly below UBSe of NT$26.44 and modestly
above consensus of NT$24.40. Growth was driven primarily by strong ASPs, up 65%
QoQ (vs UBSe of +50%) mainly on the back of the renewal of 6-month and 1-year

contracts that had expired, partially offset by lower-than-expected bit growth of -
5% QoQ (vs UBSe of +5% QoQ). Profitability, again, improved sequentially, with gross/
operating margins rising to 85.7%/80.0% from 79.5%/73.7% in 2Q26.
Fulfilment rates to remain low until Fab 5A ramp
NTC continues to see supportive supply-demand dynamics, with fulfilment rates for
demand outside of long-term contracts – which account for close to 70% of bits already

– remaining low. That  said, NTC sees limited upside to unit shipments in the near term,
given low inventory levels and limited floor space: (1) reiterating its full-year bit growth
guidance of "up high-teens % YoY," implying 4Q unit shipments will decline MSD
% QoQ (UBSe: -6%); and (2) guiding shipments to remain flat thereafter until its new
fab begins to ramp in 2H27. NTC's efforts to bring Fab 5A online are progressing well,
with equipment move-in scheduled for 1Q27 and initial production expected by 3Q27.

NTC continues to expect wafer capacity to reach 30k wpm by the end of 2028 and 50k
by the end of 2030. With demand remaining strong and existing capacity not enough to
fully address this, NTC continues to see upside to sequential ASP growth in the near
term, although at a more moderate pace.
Changes to forecasts
Reflecting 3Q26 results, as well as our new bit growth and ASP forecasts, we keep our
2026E forecasts largely unchanged but slightly raise our 2027E/2028E revenue forecasts

by 7%/5% to NT$655bn/NT$735bn (from NT$613bn/NT$702bn previously) and
EPS forecasts by 10%/7% to NT$128.16/NT$134.53 (from NT$116.41/NT$125.68
previously). Our 2027E/2028E EPS forecasts now sit 10%/8% above pre-results Visible
Alpha consensus.
Valuation: PT NT$640/share; maintain Buy
We value Nanya Tech shares at 2.63x NTM P/BV based on our 2028E-31E ROE estimate
of 21.4% and CoE of 8.1%.

### Page 2

Nanya Technology UBS ResearchNanya Tech (2408.TW)
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will DRAM undersupply continue into 2028?
Yes, in our view. Agentic AI is boosting further memory demand on multiple fronts beyond HBM,
including DDR5/LPDDR5 for conventional servers and CPU head nodes in AI servers. We now forecast
an acceleration of memory demand growth into 2027 with DRAM bit end-consumption growing

40% YoY vs 21% in 2026. As almost all of the incremental wafer capacity addition for DRAM is
going to HBM, supply is not catching up with demand. The main risk remains affordability by
customers as memory industry revenues are expected to reach US$1.69tn in 2027E.
Q: Can NTC grow as a niche DRAM player?
Yes, we think 2027 is likely to be a year of more constrained growth. Subsequently, though, we think
its process tech migration to 1c nm and 1d nm will likely lead to a 30% increase in bits per wafer for

each process tech migration (at comparable yields). NTC is also slightly pulling in its new fab ramp,
with equipment expected to move in 1Q27. We also see a tail of DDR3 and DDR4 products NTC can
address for a while.
Nanya Tech shares trade at 2.17x NTM P/BV, above +1SD of its 10-year average. That said, we believe
the strength of the unfolding DRAM cycle, as well as the tail of legacy products that Nanya can
address as a niche player, should continue to provide support for the stock.

Our industry checks suggest HBM demand continues to result in capacity allocation away from DDR,
which may moderate possible cyclical corrections, supporting upside to DDR contract pricing for
longer.
At 2.17x NTM P/BV, we believe Nanya Tech shares are discounting a LT average ROE of 17.6% vs our
LT estimate of 21.4%.

Company Description
Nanya Tech is a pure DRAM company based in Taiwan, focusing on legacy products for
consumers, broad-based applications and is now growing in servers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Nanya Tech is a pure-play DRAM manufacturer based in Taiwan mainly focused on legacy DDR

products for customers across various end applications. We think downside risks to our thesis
could include a potential slowdown in end demand and hence the conventional memory
cycle as well as Nanya's legacy-centric product portfolio. We value Nanya Tech shares using a
target NTM P/BV multiple based on long-term average ROE and cost of equity.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Monthly Databook: Lower

18 is unchanged; lower iPad

Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
We keep our 3Q26 iPhone build estimate unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2%
y/y). But we have made adjustments to new iPhone 18 builds by lowering iPhone
Duo by 0.5M units to 0.5M, while raising iPhone 18 Pro Max by 0.5M units to 8.5M.

For 4Q, we lower our iPhone build estimate by 1M units to 72M units (+33% q/q,
-5% y/y), reflecting lower legacy iPhone models (iPhone 16/17e). Total new iPhone
18 model family units remains at 71M units (-21% y/y) in 2H26, with one less SKU
launched compared to last year. We continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo
builds in 2H26. With iPhone Duo sales starting soon, we will continue to monitor
ramp-up progress for any upside or downside to builds. Our revised full-year iPhone

build forecast implies CY26 shipment at 234M units (+3% y/y).
Our 3Q26 iPad build forecast is lowered by 0.5M units to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -
18% y/y). We lowered 3Q entry level iPad builds due to  pull-forward demand in 1H
and the impact of rising prices. We also lower our 4Q iPad build forecast by 0.7M
units to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y). Our revised full-year iPad build forecast
implies CY26 shipments at 46.8M units (-14% y/y).

and supply chain checks.

Key Takeaways
3Q26e iPhone build unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2% y/y).
4Q26e iPhone build lowered slightly to 72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y).
3Q26e iPad build lowered slightly to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -18% y/y).
4Q26e iPad build lowered to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y).

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。  
增加持股‧維持

### Page 2

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。 
 A14 製程已開始進行產能準備，預計於 2027 年如期試產，並於
2028 年量產。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：台積電各製程晶圓營收比重  
台積電是全球最大的專業半 導體晶圓代工廠， 近年來其市占率
持續有所增加。 2013 年的八吋約當產能超越一千六百萬片。

廠，外加正在興建的一座十二吋廠。台積電提供以下服務：晶
圓製造，晶圓探測，組裝和測試，光罩生產和設計服務。

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

嘉澤：全球前三大 CPU Socket 供應商，伺服器已成營收主軸
全球前三大中央處理器插座CPU socket供應商。依Bishop統計在電腦暨週邊連
接器廠商中全球排名第四，不分行業別的全球連接器排名全球排名第十八。
主要客戶為AMD與Intel。產品涵蓋 CPU Socket、記憶體插槽、高速連接器及
線材，並延伸至 SOCAMM、液冷快接頭與 NPO Socket。
本研究預估，嘉澤伺服器營收由 2026 年221.14 億元增至 2027 年 328.67 億元，
年增 48.6%。成長來自板量回升、CPU新平台提高 Socket 與高速連接器價值，
以及 AI 新產品放量；PC 相關營收占比則由 2025 年 36.60%，降至 2027 年
16.5%，營運重心持續轉向伺服器。

CPU socket是甚麼
CPU 插槽的設計目的是將 CPU 處理器牢固地固定在主機板上，並在設備移動時
有助於防止潛在的損壞。嘉澤主要客戶為AMD與Intel。

針腳在CPU上，插入主
機板的孔位中。
CPU 底部是微小錫球，直
接高溫焊接在主機板上。
CPU 底部是平貼金屬觸點，
針腳做在主機板插槽上。外觀結構
可以。直接拉起拉桿即

1. 針腳在 CPU 上，輕

1. 體積最薄、最省空間。
適合行動裝置。
1.風險轉移：安裝失誤時損
壞的是主機板，CPU 價值
較高(倍數差距)

散熱表現優異。
2.高密度高上限：相同面積
內可容納更多觸點，能支援
更多通道與更高供電，技術
天花板最高。

理體積大，無法做到高
密度排列，高頻與多通
道（如 DDR5）訊號完
整性不如 LGA。

永久焊接，壞任何一個晶
片通常需要更換整張主機
板。

板插槽針腳如毛髮般細小，
清理或安裝時極易壓歪，且
幾乎無法手動修復。

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
原先：用戶提出問題；應用程式向模型發送提示；模型生成答案；應用程式將答
案傳回給用戶。主節點CPU負責調度、I/O和系統管理，而GPU則負責繁重的運
算任務。
現在：代理AI不再只是回答一個問題，而是將目標分解成多個步驟，決定下一步
該做什麼，調用多個模型，查詢資料庫，連接API，運行企業應用程序，檢查權
限，檢索內存，驗證輸出，然後再次循環執行整個過程。
CPU負責：編排：管理分解複雜任務的引擎。代理執行和工具呼叫：觸發 API 和
傳統企業軟體。策略與安全：對每個自主操作進行實際檢查。
隨 AI 從模型推論延伸至 Agent 執行，CPU 的角色擴及任務協調、工具操作及資
料處理。AMD 5 月官方文章已描述 CPU：GPU 由過去約 1：4–8，朝 1：1 移
動；Intel Q2 法說進一步表示，目前以顆數計已接近均衡。兩家原廠的觀察，支
持 CPU 在 AI 基礎建設中重要性提高

資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
5

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
AI 基礎設施正在從「以 GPU 為中心」的架構，走向「GPU 與 CPU 分工協
作」的分散式架構。未來不是只增加 GPU，而是新增獨立的 Agentic CPU
compute layer
New Agentic CPU Racks：新增的 Agent 執行層負責 Agent Control
Plane 與 Tool Execution管理 agent 的任務拆解、工作流調度、狀態管理與
多 agent 協作執行 API 呼叫、SQL 查詢、文件檢索、資料處理、程式執行與
外部工具調用處理權限控管、稽核紀錄、 安全檢查、重試與人工審批核心功
能是把 LLM 的「決策」轉換成企業系統中的「實際行動」

Meta Muse：個人代理產品化，使 CPU 執行環境的需求更具體
Muse 的投資意義在於：大型消費平台開始提供可持續執行任務的個人代理，使
CPU 沙盒需求從技術架構走向實際服務。
與AMD的關係Meta已部署數百萬顆AMD EPYC，並是Venice、Verano 的領先
客戶。因此，Meta 的運算擴張是 AMD CPU 需求的重要觀察對象。
持續在線的虛擬機（Persistent VMs）： Meta Muse 的底層採用了 Muse
Secure VM 架構。這些 VM 即使在使用者沒有主動點開 App 時，也在後台持續
運作，消耗大量的作業系統配置、記憶體管理與安全驗證資源—這些全部屬於通
用運算，只有 CPU 能做。因此當億萬名用戶的 AI 代理同時在線，CPU 的短缺
會遠比 GPU 來得更強烈。Muse 這樣的個人代理規模不斷擴大，對 CPU 的需求
勢必會增長，預計 CPU 與 GPU 的比例將從 1:1往4:1以上走 (intel電話會議)

資料來源 :trendforce, meta
對運算需求的意義Muse 是甚麼
大型平台投入代理服務產品
化
2026 年 9 月 8 日推出個人
AI 代理
執行環境需要 CPU、記憶體
及系統資源
使用專屬安全虛擬機 Muse
Secure VM
工具操作與程式執行增加具備瀏覽器，可跨應用處理工作
工作量不完全取決於即時聊
天次數
關閉 App 後仍可繼續處理

Meta muse帶動AI agent趨勢，使CPU類股上漲
 Meta Muse 在 9 月 8 日剛上線時，市場仍在觀望它與傳統的問答機器人（如 ChatGPT）
有何不同。直到 9 月 18 日（上線第 10 天），第三方研調機構 Sensor Tower 釋出數據，
顯示 Muse 已經連續數天登頂美國 App Store 免費榜冠軍，13 天內累計下載突破 250 萬
次。這時市場才驚覺，這是一個「現象級爆款」。
 Muse 可以實際幫用戶串接 Gmail、自動比價，甚至與 Shopify 的 Shop Pay 整合代客結
帳。當週一（9 月 21 日）開盤，市場資金首先推升 Meta 股價大漲 11%。CPU類股全面
大漲10%，嘉澤隔日也開盤即漲停，顯示市場有定位上的連動。(如下圖)
 截至9/30，目前該應用僅在美國與加拿大上線2 2天突破了 500 萬次下載。相比之下，
ChatGPT 達到 500 萬次下載花了 56 天，Grok 花了 103 天，Claude 則耗時 492 天。市

場發現 AI 正從「回答問題」走向「替人類實際操作」的實際應用，引發了 AI Agent的邁
向普及的轉折點。

開發NPO socket，為未來潛在商機
資料來源 :moneyDJ、利成光電科技有限公司
圖片來源：OIF 的《Co-Packaging Framework》第 10 頁、圖 2(d) 21
NPO的核心定義是：將光引擎從傳統的前面板可插拔位置，移動到更靠近交
換晶片（ASIC） 的地方，但光引擎和ASIC仍然是兩個獨立的封裝體。它們通
常被並排安裝在同一塊高性能基板（如矽中介層或有機基板）上
NPO Socket已進入客戶最後認證階段，且達可量產狀態，量產目標落在2027
年第3季，目前未納入估計，等待未來公司給更多指引。
AI資料中心在Scale-up與Scale-out兩種架構同步拉高頻寬需求，嘉澤在NPO、
既有高速銅傳輸與高功率連接方案的產品組合，將對應不同平台導入時程。

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。  
群益預估耕興 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 12.44 元 /13.73 元： 群

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

⼤族激光第三季度营收 80.61 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 58.07% ；归⺟净利润 8.89 亿元，同⽐增⻓
137.03% 。前三季度营收增⻓ 68.92% ，扣⾮净利润同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，盈利增⻓主要来⾃

摘要：
⼤族激光 2026 年前三季度营收和净利润均实现较快增⻓。财报显⽰，公司前三季度实现营业收
⼊ 214.74 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 68.92% ；归属于上市公司股东的净利润为 21.77 亿元，同⽐增⻓
152.19% 。第三季度单季营收 80.61 亿元、净利润 8.89 亿元，同⽐分别增⻓ 58.07% 和 137.03% ，
延续了上半年的增⻓势头。
盈利指标⽅⾯，前三季度扣除⾮经常性损益后的净利润为 23.01 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，⾼于
归⺟净利润增速。同期，公司基本每股收益为 2.11 元，上年同期为 0.84 元；加权平均净资产收益

率为 10.39% ，同⽐提⾼ 5.37 个百分点。
报告期内，⼤族激光⼦公司⼤族数控在⾹港联交所主板上市，募集资⾦近 50 亿元。受此影响，
公司少数股东权益由年初的 16.43 亿元增⾄ 40.87 亿元，总资产则由 382 亿元增⾄ 508.50 亿元。

### Page 2

营收与利润：收⼊增⻓带动盈利提升
前三季度，⼤族激光实现营业收⼊ 214.74 亿元，较上年同期的 127.13 亿元增加 87.61 亿元，同⽐
增⻓ 68.92% 。同期，公司营业利润为 29.77 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 189.57% ；利润总额为 29.83 亿元，
同⽐增⻓约 190% 。
从利润构成看，前三季度扣⾮净利润同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，⾼于归⺟净利润 152.19% 的增幅，表明
扣除⾮经常性损益后，公司盈利增⻓仍较为明显。报告期内，公司⾮经常性损益合计约为 -1.24
亿元，其中持有⾦融资产公允价值变动带来约 2.02 亿元损失，对当期利润形成⼀定拖累。相⽐之
下，上年同期相关公允价值变动为正贡献，影响了两期净利润增速的可⽐性。
成本⽅⾯，前三季度营业成本为 138.47 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 61.53% ，低于营业收⼊增速。销售费
⽤、管理费⽤和研发费⽤分别为 12.84 亿元、 12.21 亿元和 18.36 亿元，合计约 43.41 亿元。相关
费⽤增速低于收⼊增速，在⼀定程度上反映出收⼊规模扩⼤后费⽤占⽐有所下降，但后续仍需

结合⽑利率、费⽤率及业务结构变化判断盈利改善的持续性。
现⾦流：经营活动现⾦流转正，投资⽀出增加
前三季度，公司经营活动产⽣的现⾦流量净额为 2.27 亿元，上年同期为净流出 8.62 亿元，同⽐改
善约 10.89 亿元。同期，销售商品、提供劳务收到的现⾦为 161.75 亿元，同⽐增⻓约 57% ，经营
活动现⾦流较上年同期有所改善。
不过，经营活动现⾦流净额仍明显低于同期归⺟净利润，反映出利润与现⾦回收之间仍存在差
距。随着收⼊规模扩⼤，应收账款同步增⻓，后续回款情况仍值得关注。
投资活动⽅⾯，公司前三季度现⾦流量净额为 -13.98 亿元，上年同期为净流⼊ 7.58 亿元。现⾦流
变化与股权投资及固定资产投⼊增加有关，相关投资涉及安腾创新、永通智造、胜宏科技、景
旺电⼦等企业，以及华东区域总部基地建设。
筹资活动现⾦流量净额为 37.85 亿元，主要受⼤族数控赴港上市募资等因素影响。期末货币资⾦
余额为 98.82 亿元，较年初增加 18.69 亿元。需要注意的是，⼦公司上市募资属于资本性资⾦来

源，与主营业务经营产⽣的现⾦流性质不同。
资产负债表：规模扩张，应收账款与存货同步增⻓
截⾄报告期末，⼤族激光总资产增⾄ 508.50 亿元，较年初增⻓ 32.95% 。除经营规模扩⼤外，⼤
族数控上市带来的资⾦注⼊及少数股东权益增加，也是资产规模扩张的重要因素。

元，借款期限结构有所变化。期末资产负债率约为 48.2% ，低于年初的 50.5% 。不过，判断财务
结构变化仍需综合考察有息负债规模、偿债期限及经营现⾦流状况。

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

etc.).
B) Surge in U.S. AI-related imports, tariff front-running, shipping disruptions and fuel- price
spike feed acyclical inflation.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.

complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

are less
rational

### Page 9

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

### Page 1

The US 10yr yield gapped back below 5% with some conviction.
We're not convinced it keeps on going. More likely it ultimately
reverts up again. Brent oil pushed above $110 for the ﬁrst time
since May, but weaker market sentiment prevented a hawkish
repricing. French bond spreads continue to surge, and we
struggle to see catalysts to push against this

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
10-year French government bonds are now yielding above 5%, whereas the
US 10-year Treasury has dipped below 5%
The US 10yr back below 5% - let's see how
it feels down there
The US 10yr yield broke back below 5% around midday in New York
trading on Thursday. And did so with a degree of conviction. We
thought 5% could hold, but always acknowledged that a
heightened (and still heightening) risk-oﬀ tone could in the end
dominate, at least for the near term. The fact that the French 10yr
has gone the other way and shot above 5% in recent days, and the
spread out to 175bp vs Germany, is also of relevance. We also note

### Page 2

that Thursday was the ﬁrst day of pronounced ﬂight into Bunds
since the French wobbles took hold. Treasuries have shared in this.
Whether we remain below 5% is a whole other question. We're
minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still have inﬂation /
ﬁscal deﬁcit / AI boom underpinnings to navigate. And we have the
US mid-term elections approaching as an excuse for trouble. But it
will need some degree of calm in the risk space ﬁrst. What happens

on Friday is anyone's guess, and it may well be that longs in cash
bond duration oﬀer comfort to most through an uncertain
weekend. We'll see. We still think the 10yr yield gets back above 5%
ultimately. The timing for that is a tougher call.
It's also true that there has been a tendency shown in recent weeks
to buy into concessional 5+% levels for the US 10yr. We saw that in
spades post the recent poor CPI number, which was followed by an
immediate sell-oﬀ in Treasuries, but then an even bigger

subsequent rally. In a way, the latest break below 5% is a
culmination of that move. Now that 5% has been breached below,
where next?
More pertinently, can the market ﬁnd comfort at sub 5% levels
given the contemporaneous inﬂation dynamic. And, can the
market really ignore the fact that neither side has a plan for the
ﬁscal deﬁcit as we roll into the mid-term elections in the next
couple of weeks. One thing is for sure: the risk-oﬀ environment
takes pressure oﬀ the Fed to hike next week. But even given that,

we'd not be loading up on duration here, at least not just yet.
French downgrade lurking and risk
sentiment not helping French spreads either
Amid all this, French bonds remain in the line of ﬁre with the spread
over Bunds widening at times by 15bp, with the spread touching
177bp. That is still below its late-2011 peak of just under 200bp,
but at this pace it is only a matter of days before we see a new
high. Although a lot has changed in the eurozone since 2011, some

may consider Italy’s spreads during the sovereign debt crisis the
better yardstick since it was the country in market focus back then
and not France.

That said, the latest widening seems to be more driven by the
deteriorating general sentiment, as there were no signiﬁcant news
headlines out of France. But the prospect of ongoing uncertainty
until the presidential elections keeps investors sidelined. And French
bonds can’t really beneﬁt from any recovery in general sentiment,
leaving spreads at wider levels. For now, it feels like watching a train
wreck in slow motion where we have not seen the end of the

widening yet.
One event that market observers have been looking forward to is
the scheduled rating review by Moody’s after markets close on
Friday. Moody’s still has France on Aa3 with a negative outlook, and
therefore one notch above the other two major rating agencies that
place France already at A+, even with stable outlooks for France. A
downgrade from Moody’s now looks overdue and should be
considered a catch-up. Commentary will be parsed for further

downgrade triggers, since the developments in France – in terms of
political stability, public resistance and reﬁnancing costs – oﬀer
little hope of improvement anytime soon. In terms of timing, note
that there is no ﬁxed release time. When Moody’s downgraded
France in 2024, the news hit the wires only just after midnight CET.
Having at least this one risk event out of the way might bring about
some stabilisation. But any lasting relief would need positive news.

A budget for 2027 with at least some semblance of ﬁscal
consolidation would be a start, but might also have to wait until
December.
Friday’s events and market view
PMI data on Friday will be the next test for markets. So far,
both equity markets and economic data point to a resilient
economic outlook, but this narrative is challenged by rising
rates and higher oil prices. Concerns about AI valuations are
a recurring daily theme while energy prices keep drifting

higher. If we were to also see headwinds materialising in the
economic data, we could face quite a bullish repricing in
rates. Especially for euro rates, a more downbeat growth

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

China

Exports growth is expected to remain 
robust at 25.7% y oy in September, 
supported by continued strength in 
electronics demand. Both the official and 
Caixin manufacturing PMIs rose above 
the 50 expansion threshold during the 
month, pointing to improving 
manufacturing activity. External trade 
indicators also stren gthened, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage 
handled at China's 20 major ports 
accelerating from -0.7% yoy in August to 
7.2% in September. Freight activity 
improved as well, as growth in 
international cargo flights picked up 
from -1.4% y oy to 3.6% over the same 
period.

On the price front, factory -gate and raw 
material prices rebounded amid the 
renewed escalation in geopolitical 
tensions. Main raw material purchasing 
price and producer price subPMI 
increased from 56.6 and 50.4 to 60.8 and 
54.0 during the period. However, 
downstream consumer price pressures 
are likely to remain relatively subdued 
under the pricing-band mechanism. As a 
result, CPI inflation is expected to edge 
up from 0.8% y oy in August to 1.0% in 
September. Nevertheless, soft domestic 
demand is expected to continue 
constraining the pace of consumer price 
increases.

India

September inflation is likely to gather 
momentum to 5.7% y/y, partly driven by 
base effects, from 4.8% the month 
before. Food segments continue to 
register gains, with pressures 
broadening to include perishables, 
edible oils, rice, pulses, sugar, etc., 
according to high-frequency data. Under 
non-food, retail pump prices have not 
been adjusted further, though other fuel 
products, such as non -subsidised LPG, 
diesel and ATF (still up by double digits 
y/y, though slower than in August), are 
up on the year. Core  inflation is also 
likely to tick up, aligning with the central 
bank's view that price risks are no longer 
benign. Pressures have built up due to 
the overhang of an uneven and sub -par 
monsoon, drought conditions in parts of 
the country, and elevated oil pr ices. In 
the face of higher headline prints, we 
expect the RBI to keep the door open for 
another rate hike in December 2026.

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
Date:    3 hours ago
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question
For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
By Ed Yardeni • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

The risks to the Fed’s dual mandate are constantly changing. Its attempt
to hit its inflation target is complicated by price pressures beyond the
control of interest rates, and its attempt to hit on a federal funds rate that
neither over-stimulates nor over-restricts economic growth is complicated
by the elusive nature of this neutral rate. Today, Elias and Ed examine the
forces with bearing on the Fed’s rate decisions since 2022. They also
explain why they believe that the neutral federal funds rate has risen over
recent years and the implications for monetary policy looking ahead.

《醫療器械喜憂參半，但必要性手術需求仍佳》
近月醫療服務設備股為健護中股價表現較差 ，主因：
1)亞培受到產品責任險達成部分和解協議 ，後續還
有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。
